# The desktop

A Tauri 2 shell around a React 19 app, over the node it spawns as a sidecar. The shell holds what
has the blast radius of a machine — the PTY, the file dialogs, the login environment; the app holds
screens, and every fact a screen computes lives in a pure `.mjs` model with a test. This page is the
map; [`desktop/README.md`](../../../desktop/README.md) is how it is built and
[guide/the-desktop.md](../../guide/the-desktop.md) is what each surface is for.

---

## Where things live

| Directory | Owns |
|---|---|
| `desktop/src/` root | `App.tsx` (the shell that never unmounts; it marks nothing read — the thread does, 13-conversations §Lists, search, coming back), `main.tsx` (the shell's boundary, and the `node:restarted` listener that `forgetApiBase()`s so a port that moved is resolved again on the next call), `router.ts`, `api.ts`, `bus.ts`, `types.ts` / `types.gen.ts` / `types.hand.ts`, `log.ts` + `logModel.mjs` (the webview's logger — `log.error/warn/info/debug(target, message, fields)`, one door for every surface, to the shell's `log_event` command in the desktop and to the console in a browser; `configureLog` the machine's `logging.*` in force, forwarded to the shell's file layer through `log_configure`; `installGlobalLogHandlers` the uncaught error, the unhandled rejection and the page going away (*page hidden* at `info`, so a file that ends without it says the webview died without a word), once, from `main.tsx`; the outbox — one `invoke` after another, so the lines land in the order they were said; the model: the level words held equal to the Rust crate's, `passes`, `configFrom`, `shapeEvent` — a message cut, at most sixteen fields each JSON-safe and cut — and `errorFields`, an error's name, message, status, path, code and a few stack lines, never a body), `activity.ts` + `activityModel.mjs` (`guidedWords(phase, status, detail)` is the one sentence table the live `guided` event and the `guidance` fact share; `guardWords(tool, verdict, by, rule, reason, subject)` the same for the live `guard_decided` event and the `guard` fact — a refusal is a failure, a question a wait, an allow ambient), `askModel.mjs`, `styles.css` (which leaves out the bare utilities a bundled widget's class names would collide with — `@source not inline("{visible,invisible,contents,container}")`, held by `theme/widgetClasses.test.mjs`), the guard tests `noHappyDom.test.mjs` and `localStorageKeys.test.mjs` |
| `shell/` | chrome that outlives every screen: `workstreamFramesModel.mjs` (which engine facts move a workstream — its record, or what the node says of its checkout — the one list the status store and the Workstreams panel's hook read); `latestModel.mjs` (`createLatest`: one answer at a time and the newest asked for — a document's reads, a link's clicks — none landing on a reader that left); `liveCount.ts` (`createLiveCount`: a count of one kind of record the bus keeps fresh — what the notes and drawings docks wear — read whole when something first shows it, once per burst of the frames the caller's `moves` names, and again when the bus comes back; the first listener starts it and the last stops it, the last count kept so a dock hidden and shown paints it at once; answers in order through `createLatest`, held through strict mode's remount); `linkCardModel.mjs` (the card at the pointer, ide/17: the roots a text is read against when its surface names none, a path's verbs per resolution — open, open in each checkout, reveal, open as a loose file, copy — a URL's — the embedded browser first, the machine's, copy; only http and https open — and the copy's words; `linkHandler.tsx` binds each verb to its act and draws — a root's path from the workspace or `GET /placement`, an `unlisted` guess confirmed by `GET /tree` before the card, each answer held to the click's ticket); `SetupGate.tsx` and `useReadiness.ts` over `setupModel.mjs` (the setup gate, [16](../16-setup-gate.md) — `useReadiness` reading `GET /readiness` on mount, on a fact that moves the checks (`movesReadiness`: `settings_changed`), after a fix, on *Check again*, when the bus comes back after the node was away, and on the model's cadence (`recheckMs`: every twenty seconds while something is missing **or while the read failed**, so a node not yet up when the desktop opened never leaves the gate unknown for the life of the window); what a read leaves the gate knowing is `afterRead` — one that failed keeps the last answer and says why, in the log too; `gateMode` nothing · banner over Settings and Agents (`bannerWords`) · the modal elsewhere, an alert dialog Escape does not close; a card per check with `checkWords`, this platform's official lines (`platformOf`, `commandsFor`) each with Copy, *Open official docs* through `openExternal`, the door (`doorTarget`, `doorLabel`) and the fixes as one call each (`offeredFixes`, `fixBody` → `api.setSettings` / `api.patchAgent`; a fix that names no agent, no harness, no plan or no setting is no call and no button); `progressWords`), `motion.ts` (`prefersReducedMotion()` — the one place the desktop asks; the pet's sprite stands still, a streamed reply is shown as it lands), `DeviceLauncher.tsx` (ide/19 — **Devices** beside Browser on a Flutter checkout while mobile development is on: the main click shows the device the app runs on, else runs on the one that is up, else boots one, else the setup; the caret every device's verbs by state, then *Check the setup…*; `openTerminalIn({mobile: {device}})` names the device and nothing else), `devicesStore.ts` (the devices the node lists, read once and again on `mobile_development_changed`, single-flight; `useDevices(load)`, `refreshDevices`, `devicesNow`), `deviceShots.ts` (a device's screen captured by the node — `takeDeviceShot`, copied, saved, or attached as a whole-screen `capture` chip); sidebar (`Sidebar.tsx` over `sidebarModel.mjs`: the seven destinations in the person's order (`navOrderStore.ts` over `navOrderModel.mjs` — `orderKeys` makes a stored order whole against `PRIMARY_NAV`, `placeKey` moves one, `homeKey` names the first — `homeRoute()` is the router's home — kept per viewer under `bisa.sidebar.order`; the rows and the rail's doors are each one `SortableList` carrying `navRowDrag`, the palette's *Go to* reads the same `usePrimaryNav()`; *Reset order* under Settings › Appearance) — the Inbox row's badge `inboxBadge`, everything not yet dealt with, accented by what is owed — the live sections — channels (`general` first, then creation order) and direct channels (the one that moved last first), each through `channelListModel.sortChannels` / `sortDms`, the same order the Channels and Messages pages keep, one *Hosted by …* section per workspace this node is a guest of (`hostedModel.mjs`) — an offline card and nothing else at its foot; a conversation is reached where it is about, never from here; two modes, `expanded` and `collapsed` (`readMode` · `toggledMode` · `toggleWords`, remembered as `bisa.sidebar.mode`), the collapsed one `SidebarRail.tsx` — `railDoors`: the same doors as icons, then Channels and Messages with their unread summed, each with `railTooltip` as its name, the Inbox count in its icon's corner (`CountBadge` `size="sm"`, which caps at *9+* — `ui/badgeModel.mjs`: `BADGE_MAX` by size, 99 for a row's badge and 9 for a mark over an icon, `badgeText`; the exact numbers are the door's tooltip), `RAIL_WIDTH` wide with no resize handle; the rail's geometry is three numbers in the model — `RAIL_WIDTH` 44, `RAIL_DOOR` 32, `RAIL_OVERHANG` 4 — and one rule, `railGutter`: a door's marks (the count, the *agent is writing* dot, the active bar) hang `RAIL_OVERHANG` outside the door, into the gutter beside it, so the doors' `<nav>` — which scrolls when the rail is long, and a box that scrolls on one axis clips on both — spans the rail's whole width (`w-full`); shrunk to its doors it clipped every count at the door's edge, and `sidebarModel.test.mjs` holds the classes to the numbers; the toggle between the two is the top chrome's alone — the rail draws none), top chrome (`ProfileMenu` last on the bar — the person's identicon, whose menu is *Identity*, *Settings*, *About Bisa*; `AboutDialog` over `aboutModel.mjs`: this desktop's version from `__APP_VERSION__`, Vite's `define` of package.json's, the node's from `GET /health`, the data directory, the npub, a caution when the two versions differ; the platform's mark at 40 px beside the name), omnibox, `harnessUsageStore.ts` (what each harness's account has left, by harness id — read while a surface shows it, refreshed every `USAGE_POLL_MS` while any does, paused otherwise; `refreshHarnessUsage` asks the node's source again; a re-read that fails keeps the last report an hour with the reason as `stale`, `harnessUsageModel.settled`) with `HarnessUsageLine.tsx` over `harnessUsageModel.mjs` (`UsageLine`, the paint over an entry — the windows in the adapter's order, each *word · `Meter` · percentage* with its own reset in its tooltip, `StackedBar` (bands sharing one whole — each a tone of its own, never the track's, a hairline between, a two-pixel floor for a sliver — and a legend of words beneath; the footer's resource and browser overlays draw theirs with it), the first window's reset in place, the credit balance after a divider on the full line only, a refresh door, the plan and the source in the tooltip, dimmed when the report is the kept one, or the harness's own sentence; `HarnessUsageLine`, the folded line Settings › Harnesses shows — the rail's and the Workstreams panel's harness rows carry none; the footer is where an account is read — with `UsageToggle`, the gauge, per harness on the fold key `usage.<harness>`), `UsageStat.tsx` (the footer's left: the pinned harness's mark, name and `UsageLine` compact — `5h ▰▰ 24% · resets in 2 h 10 min  Weekly ▰▱ 41%  Fable ▱▱ 9%` — the trigger of a `Popover` holding `UsagePicker.tsx` — every installed harness with its line, each a radio that pins it, *Refresh all*) over `footerUsageStore.ts` (`bisa.footer.usage`) and `footerUsageModel.mjs` (`pickerRows`, `pinnedHarness` — the pin while it is still installed, else the first, else none — `usageStatWords`), `portsStore.ts` (the listening ports a shell or harness opened, polled while anything can own one), `systemPermissions.ts` (the two permission commands, `null` off the shell), the terminal layer (`TerminalPanel.tsx`, `TerminalLauncher.tsx`, `TerminalStripControls.tsx`, `BrowserLauncher.tsx` (the one Browser button beside Terminal — ide/18 — over `views/_workbench/serversModel.mjs`: a tab on the newest server, port or blank; its menu *New tab · From folder…*, the servers up with *Open :port* and *Stop*, the ports, the open tabs, the page's annotation for an agent; ⌘⇧R the run door — the newest server, the run command, else the folder picker), `ServeFolderDialog.tsx` (*From folder…* — Root, then the checkout's folders as the explorer lists them, a filter over the path index, *index.html* and *serving :port* marks, the button worded by the choice; every rule `views/_workbench/serveFolderModel.mjs`'s), `serveDoors.ts` (`serveAndOpen(wid, folder)` — the one way a folder is served and opened, the picker's and the Files tree's *Serve this folder* alike: the server already up opened rather than a second asked for, the folder remembered per checkout under `bisa.ide.serve.folder`; `lastServedFolder`), `useRunCommand.ts` (the project's run command read once for the Terminal caret's *Run* item and the Browser's ⌘⇧R — `useRunCommand(wid)`, re-read on `settings_changed`; `runTheCommand` opens the terminal when approved, else says so and opens About › Settings), `useServing.ts` (every server the node has up and this checkout's slice, the ports the rail attributes — one read for every surface), `browserChromeModel.mjs` (what a tab's bar shows: a browser's bar whole on every tab — back and forward by the webview's own history, reload or Stop, the address, the wand, the camera, the doors — each verb free or held with its reason, `HELD`), `omniboxUrlModel.mjs` (an address typed into the palette as one row that opens it), `BrowserPanel.tsx` (the browser layer: one native webview per tab of `useBrowsers.ts`, laid over the host that shows its tab — the workbench's centre or the Browser pane, `layerSlots.ts` keeping one slot per host and `browserPlacementModel.mjs` saying which wins, the centre first — and hidden while any kit surface is open, `ui/openSurfaces.ts`; a placement the shell refuses is logged, never swallowed), `browserShown.ts` (whether a tab renders right now and the one wait until it does — over the hosts' slots and the surfaces' count alike, at once when it already shows — that `browserShots.takeShot` and so the camera and the bridge alike pay before a snapshot; `takeShot` refuses as `NotShownError` when the tab never shows; the one file that opens, moves and closes the webviews of `browser/session.ts`; it feeds `browserPrefsStore.ts` — the `browser.*` settings as the shell reads them — and hears the agents' `browser_request` frames, handing them to `browserBridge.ts`, which performs each through `browserBridgeModel.mjs` — asking a page comes to its answer, its silence or the tab gone (`askPage`, three outcomes; `TAB_GONE` and `PAGE_SILENT` two sentences), an act that cannot move the page is asked once more after a silence (`askAgain`, the plan's `navigates` the boundary) and an act that can never is, every refusal is one warn line with the id and the act, and the second missed presence read in a row is a warning (`presenceLapsed`) — a new tab at home where the engine said the session speaks (`scope.home`, one of the desktop's scopes), else the IDE's root, else the workspace, shown to the person by `revealPlan` — in the IDE's strip when at home with documents in the centre, else the pane — through the one door `browserDoors.showBrowserTab`, the centre read from `views/_workbench/workbenchCentreStore.ts`; a read, a snapshot, a find, a click, typing, a key, a choice, a hover, a scroll, a wait, the console and a script handed to the page's driver by ref or selector; an act that may move the page raced against the navigation it starts and answered as the new page (`navigatedResult`); back, forward, reload and a wait for the load the bridge's own — never a tab the engine keeps out of sight (`headless` on the frame and the parked row: `browsersModel` keeps it on the session, opens it without making it active, `reveal`/`revealBrowserTab` is the person's one act that shows it, `seenSessions`/`seenRootedAt` are what the strips ride; `browserPlacementModel.offstageRect` is where such a tab renders, `placementOf` answering `offstage` for it, a screenshot taken there); `BrowserPanel` reads the parked list every `BROWSER_PRESENCE_MS` so the engine knows a desktop is home; a screenshot of a tab shown first, taken by `browserShots.ts` and uploaded as an attachment, `browserShotModel.mjs` reading the PNG's size; a read or an act on any page the tab shows — and answers `POST /browser/requests/{id}` with the wire's `BrowserResult` built key by key (`browserBridgeModel.wireResult`: nothing the type does not declare, nested facts by their own keys, never `path`), and relays a browser chord pressed inside a page (`bisa:key`) as a `BROWSER_COMMAND` to the tab's bar; `browserInspectorStore.ts` holds each tab's wand and lost badges and hands the notes typed in the page's own box to its listeners; `browsersModel.mjs` the tabs' rules — the seven homes (`BROWSER_SCOPES`), `normalizeUrl`, `openBrowser` — by somebody: `PERSON`, `byAgent`, `byPage`, a target that names no opener opening nothing (`openerOf`, `openerWords`) — `navigated` with the webview's history, `titled`, `closeBrowser`, `closeAll` (the sequence kept), `browserLabel`, `draftScopeOf`, `serializeBrowsers`/`parseBrowsers` under `bisa.browser.sessions.v3` (the tabs in sight and who opened each); `browserDoorsModel.paneToggle` behind `browserDoors.toggleBrowserPane`, the person's door to the pane — the pane itself opens no tab; `browserPlacesModel.mjs` over `browserPlaces.ts` names a tab's home from the workspace's rows — `ORIGINS`, `whereWords`, `groupTabs`, `footerBrowserWords` — for the footer's count (§The footer's count in ide/18), a screen's Browser button and the pane's strip alike; `BrowserStat.tsx` is the footer's Browser read-out — one glyph and one number for every tab, tinted while a host draws one, the working dot while an agent browses — over `browserStatModel.mjs` (`statWords`, `visibilityBar`, `overlayRows`, `emptyWords`, `footnote`, the three dimensions' words and glyphs), its `Popover` holding `BrowserOverlay.tsx` in the resource overlay's shape: the bar, a `SegmentedControl` of Tabs · Origins · Unseen remembered under `browser` in `resourceDimensionStore.ts`, rows that are doors to the pane on a tab with a ✕ beside the button, *New tab* and the door to Settings › Capabilities › Browser in the header, the out-of-sight policy as the footnote; `browserDoorsModel.screenHome` is where a tab opened beside the screen is at home; `useBrowsers.ts` settles a load nobody finished after `LOAD_GRACE_MS` and says so once; `browserPlacementModel.placedAsAsked` compares the box the shell drew with the box asked, and the layer logs a stray one once per tab)), the aux pane, the window footer (`StatusBar.tsx` over `statusBarModel.mjs` — on the left `UsageStat`, what a harness's account has left; on the right the caret, the open terminals and running harnesses (`footerSessionsModel.mjs` — the two rosters reconciled through `claimedSessions`/`isDrawn`, the rail's rule, every row with where it stands from one index over the workspace (`placeIndex`, `placeWords`: *Bisa › feat/a*, the workstream's word the cards' `cardTitle`), a tab open while not exited; each row a door through `sessionDoors.ts`, the one way anything opens a terminal tab — focus, then the route with `?doc=terminal:<key>` — or a harness session — its tab, else followed with the Agents pane on its workstream; the rail's rows go through the same two functions), every open port (with `portsSummary` above the groups and each port's pid), then the node as `NodeStat.tsx` — the glyph and a dot in the connection's tone, no text, the sentence as the tooltip and the accessible name — over `NodeOverlay.tsx`, read on open and never at rest: the connection (the address, run by this app with its pid, how long up, restarts — the shell's `node_status` — or by a person), the node (`GET /node`: its version beside the desktop's with `aboutModel.mjs`'s caution, paused or running from `GET /pause`, the sessions live, the socket, where it listens, the data and logs folders), its sync from the workspace store, its own load from the stats store's node share, and the door to Settings › Node; every word `nodeStatModel.mjs`'s (`statWords`, `sinceWords`, `overlaySections`, `nodeShare`, `footnote`) — and the machine's CPU, GPU (only when the host reports one), memory and the platform's disk footprint as `ResourceStat.tsx`, the same look as the trigger of a `Popover` holding `ResourceOverlay.tsx`; `PetStat.tsx` — the footer's pet glyph, tinted while a pet shows, a `Popover` holding `PetOverlay.tsx` over `petOverlayModel.mjs`: a *Show the pet* switch (`petStore.togglePet` — the last pet shown, else Moonrice), a size slider, every pet as a small `PetTile`, *Reset position*, a door to Settings › Pet — where a bare toggle used to show or hide and nothing else about the pet was within reach: the metric's sentence, the kit's `StackedBar` of the host's use — Bisa's parts first, then the machine in the border tone, the track for what is free, a legend beneath — a `SegmentedControl` of the metric's dimensions, each with its glyph (`dimensionIcon`, a key of `ui/icons.ts` — the platform's stack, the concept's own mark for the rest) and `stretch`ed to the panel — each segment its word plus an equal share of the slack, never cut; the popover's width is the row's, forty type-rems so it grows with the text — the choice per metric kept by `resourceDimensionStore.ts` under `bisa.footer.resources` — and the rows of the chosen one with a quiet `Meter` each and a door where a row names a goal, a project (opened as its primary workstream), a workflow or a tab; every fact is `resourceModel.mjs`'s (`attributeShares` joins the shell's `ProcessShare`s to the rosters — a session by pid, a tab by its PTY id, a tab that claims a session folded into it — `platformRows`, `usageBar` (the header bar read left to right — Bisa's parts, the machine, the free rest — with its legend and its sentence; `percentWords`), `dimensionRows`, `foldRows`, `metricWords`, `footnote`, and the disk's `areas`, `goalsDisk`, `projectsDisk`, `harnessesDisk`, `terminalsDisk`, `activityRows`, all behind `overlayRows`) reading `statsStore.ts`/`statsApi.ts`; then the network as `NetworkStat.tsx` — the glyph, a dot and one word, *DOWN* (red) while this Mac cannot reach the internet, else *VPN* while a tunnel is up, else *UP*, a dash before the first read, the sentence on hover — over `NetworkOverlay.tsx`: the internet with its latency, public IP and whether DNS answers, the VPN's standing and the up tunnel's rows, every interface that is up, this Mac's default route and resolver, the proxy System Settings names, what the platform leaves through with its problems, and the door to Settings › Network; every word `networkStatModel.mjs`'s (`statWords(facts, status, online)` — the window's `navigator.onLine` beating a stale read when it says offline, standing in off the shell — `overlaySections`, `footnote`, over the Settings panel's own `views/_settings/networkModel.mjs`), the facts `networkStore.ts`'s; and the pet/notes toggles), `shortcuts.ts` + `keymapModel.mjs` + `keyContexts.ts` (the scopes a key is live in, one reading for the window listener and a terminal's key reservation; `document` inside an editor, a rendering, a conflict document or a document's own root, `[data-document]`; `designer` while `[data-designer-screen]` is on screen, whose `data-designer-owner` names the workflow or goal the mode switch's chord acts on — `designerOwnerOnScreen`) + `modeMemoryModel.mjs` (a mode remembered per key — newest last, capped, read back only when real, the next in a cycle, the settings' default clamped — the rules the IDE's centre and the Workflow screen share) + `editMenu.ts` over `editMenuModel.mjs` (the native Edit menu's verbs replayed as their chords where the focus is, ide/15) (+ `useKeymap.ts` and `CommandHint.tsx` — every chord a screen shows is read from the live keymap; every door — the creation doors, the workbench's tab verbs, ⌘N's untitled document and ⌘O, the rail's import and rename, Git's stash and history search — goes through `fire` and is listened at through `onDoor` over `doorModel.mjs`, which counts the listeners so `fire` dispatches and keeps a request fired before its listener mounted until it does, for `DOOR_TTL_MS`, so a lazy screen's chunk never loses the press that opened it; a raw window listener never serves a door, since `fire` dispatches only to a listener it knows of), `pathIndexStore.ts` (the path index per root, fetched once through the caller-independent single flight `singleFlight.mjs` and patched from `file_changed`, shared by quick open and the Files search), `notifications.ts` over `notificationsModel.mjs` (the one OS-notification door — every notice, the app's own and an addon's included, passes `deliver`, which asks the permission once and applies `allowed`: the person's master and category switches, `notifications.*`, followed by `notifySettings.ts` the way `closeGuardSettings.ts` follows its keys; the model names the moment and its category — asks · failures · done · workflows · addons · app — never the person's wish; the Dock badge is `useTray.ts`'s), `settingsStore.ts` (the settings registry read once per session and kept, and the resolved values kept per key — the workspace's or a project's — read on the first ask, read again on every `settings_changed` and on `reloadResolved`, committed only when a value moved so a control memoised on them re-renders only then; `useSettingsRegistry`, `useResolvedSettingsRead` as a panel's read, and `useResolvedSettings`, the one hook every screen reads a setting through, `shell/useResolvedSettings.ts` re-exporting it), `logSettings.ts` (`useLogSettings`, mounted once by `App`: the `logging.*` settings read at boot and on every `settings_changed` naming one, handed to `log.ts`), `useCloseGuard.ts` (the one way out — the window's close and ⌘Q, which the shell holds and hands over as `bisa:quit-requested`: ask when `desktop.confirm_quit` says so, save dirty documents, then `destroy` or `quit_app`), `closeGuardModel.mjs` (whether a close asks — `confirmsClose` over the three switches — the words each question says, and the door to its switch, `CONFIRM_DOORS`), `closeGuardSettings.ts` (the switches followed, the `logSettings` way), `terminalCloseGuard.ts` (`askClose`, the one pending question `CloseConfirmDialog` draws), theme, and the module stores (`useTerminals.ts`, `layerSlots.ts`, `sessionsStore.ts` (the roster: seeded once from `GET /sessions`, moved by `session_state` / `session_gone`, a 60 s safety refetch), `followedSessionStore.ts` over `followedSessionModel.mjs` (the session each workstream is about — chosen by an Agent panel row, a rail agent row or a harness tab at the centre, else the loudest live; session-only; what the pet follows, with the opener the workbench registers for the pet's click), `useWorkspaceData.ts` (the workspace's facts for every screen — `photoOf(pubkey)`, the face a principal wears, the owner's row included; `nameOf` your own name once you set it, *You* until then — and `markRead(scope, host)`, the one door a thread reads itself through: this node's unread and inbox row, or a hosted entry, patched at once, the node confirming after), `useHarnesses.ts` (which harnesses are installed, read once per window and shared; read again when the bus comes back after the node was away, on the model's backoff after a read that failed or found no harness a person could launch, and on `reloadHarnesses` — what Refresh on the footer's usage asks first), `harnessListModel.mjs` (`catalogIncomplete`, `nextCatalogRead` — 10 s, 30 s, 60 s, then nothing; `CATALOG_RETRY_MS`), `nav.ts` — `PRIMARY_NAV`, seven destinations: Inbox, Agents, Teams, Projects, Workflows, Goals, Pulse; `AuxPane.tsx`'s fifth occupant `artifact` (`?aux=artifact&auxId=<message>:<ordinal>`), drawn by the shell itself as `ArtifactPane.tsx` (ide/12 — the message loaded by id, the viewer with *Expand* and, in the IDE, *Open as a tab*, the gallery **In this conversation** grouped by title with versions, the stage on `?stage=1`), over `artifactSettings.ts` (`useArtifactLibraries` — the one setting a page reads); `linkHandler.tsx` — the `LinkProvider` every path and URL opens through (ide/17): the roots described from the workspace and the path index, the resolution, and the card at the pointer — *Open src/a.rs:42* in the IDE with the line parked for the editor, *Open in …* per checkout, *Reveal in Finder*, *Copy the path*; the host, the URL, *Open in Bisa's browser* (ide/18), *Open in the machine's browser* and *Copy the URL*); every shell pane — the sidebar, the top chrome, the status bar, the Details pane, the terminal panel — and the workbench's right panel is marked `data-pane`, the one attribute `theme/material.css` frosts on a glass family; `networkApi.ts` (this Mac's network facts — the VPN, its tunnel, the route, the resolvers, the proxy System Settings names — from the shell's `network_facts`, `null` off the shell); `useTray.ts` over `trayModel.mjs` (the platform's presence outside the window — [The menu bar icon](../../guide/the-desktop.md#the-menu-bar-icon): `trayReport(facts)` ranks the state — *trouble* › *connecting* › *waiting* › *working* › *paused* › *quiet* — counts the Inbox's rows that need the person (`inboxBadge(...).needs`, a waiting harness once) and the agents at work, and says both in words; the hook sends the report to the shell's `tray_report` when it changes and wears the same count on the Dock badge; it hears `tray:go` (the menu's needs line — the Inbox) and `tray:dock` (the menu's *Show in Dock* — recorded as `desktop.dock_icon`); `closeVerb(prefs)` — hide or quit — is what the red button means), `trayPrefs.ts` (the two `desktop.*` menu bar switches followed as `closeGuardSettings.ts` follows the confirmations, and `tray_dock` applied after every load: the setting is the Dock's one truth), `useCloseGuard.ts` (both ways out, held by the shell: `bisa:close-requested` hides the window or runs the flow by `closeVerb`; `bisa:quit-requested` runs the flow; every quit ends in `quit_app`), `documentGuard.ts` over `leaveGuardModel.mjs` + `UnsavedDialog.tsx` (a floating editor's **leave guard**: the editor *holds* its document — dirty, save now, let go — through `useDocumentHold`, which also registers the hold as a dirty source of `editorRegistry` so the quit question counts a note or a drawing like a document; every way out of the panel store goes through `guard.leave`, which parks the departure while the hold is dirty and the panel draws the one *Save · Don't save · Cancel* question — `UnsavedDialog`, the workbench's tab guard drawn from the same file; the same box whatever the state, so rows never jiggle; `sessionGlyph` resolves the vocabulary's `icon:*` against `ICON`), `SessionStateChip` (the same glyph beside the word), `sessionState.mjs` (the nine-word session vocabulary every surface reads, its glyph per word, and `gateOf`), `sessionMotion.mjs` (`motionOf`, `repeats`, `motionClass` — the motion vocabulary, every class stopped under reduced motion in `theme/motion.css`), `PlatformMark.tsx` (the platform's **own** mark — the one file `logo/logo.svg` at the repository root, a blue glass squircle holding the split B, shown as an `<img>` with a harness mark's props so a call site holding a `Mark` does not care which it got — `tone` accepted and ignored, the artwork carries its own colour; the same file is what `just app-icon` rasterises into the OS icon set, so nothing in code draws or generates a mark), `harnessMarks.tsx` (one inline-SVG mark per harness, the harness's **own**: Claude Code, Codex, GitHub Copilot, Grok, Gemini, Goose and pi from LobeHub's MIT set, OpenCode and Cursor from Simple Icons (CC0), Oh My Pi hand-drawn since it publishes none, an ACP plug, a custom wand — GitHub Copilot CLI, Grok Build and Gemini CLI speak ACP under ids of their own and wear their own marks, never the plug (`harnessMarkModel.test.mjs` holds every built-in id of the Rust catalog to a mark of its own) — each taking a lucide icon's props and a `tone="brand"`; `harnessMark(id)` resolves a harness id to its mark or the terminal glyph; the licences in `NOTICES.md`), `modelWords.mjs` (a session's model as a row's words — the provider prefix folded away, the full id for the tooltip, nothing guessed) over `harnessMarkModel.mjs` (`markIdOf` folds `acp:*`, `custom:*` and the `preset:*` ids the catalog names to one mark each), `placeholderChips.mjs` (a redacted secret an agent quoted back — `«secret:kind:tag»` — rendered by `Markdown` as a chip naming its kind, the placeholder in the tooltip), `artifact/` (ide/12 — the artifact viewers: `ArtifactView` (the toolbar and, under it, `KindView`), `KindView` (the one renderer per kind — the switch the artifact viewer and the IDE's rendered documents share), `fileBytes.ts` (`useIdeFileBytes`: a checkout file's bytes through `GET /ide/raw` with the bearer header, a blob URL revoked on leave, *too large* from the 413), `ArtifactCard` (under a message — a picture inline, a page live at a bounded height and at most three at once, the rest a poster), `ArtifactStage` (over the window), `PageFrame` (the sandboxed frame with the page's own policy from `pageDocument.mjs`, the inspector from `pageInspector.mjs` when the IDE annotates the page, dressed by `inspectorTheme.mjs` (the role tokens `inspectorTokens.ts` reads off `<html>`, mapped to every part's `cssText`; baked into the script as the page opens, said again as `bisa:theme` when the theme moves) — and, in a browser tab, the driver the agents' tools speak to (ide/18): a snapshot of the page's outline with a ref per element, a read as text or HTML, a find, a click, typing a keystroke at a time, a key pressed, an option chosen, a hover, a scroll, a wait on a `MutationObserver`, the console and the errors kept from the load, the dialogs answered and reported, a script evaluated;
