| `i18n/` | every word the desktop says, in the language the window speaks ([17](../17-internationalisation.md)): `l10n.mjs` (the one door — `t(id, args)`, `attr(id, name, args)`, `tx(text)` for a `Text` the node sent; the catalog installed once; a missing message renders as its id and is told once through `onMissing`; isolation marks off), `rich.tsx` (`rich(id, slots)` — a sentence with an element inside it, the slot a `<name/>` tag in the message), a file with a `t` of its own imports the door as `tr`, `localeModel.mjs` (`AVAILABLE` — the shipped languages, held equal to `bisa-i18n`'s by a guard — `negotiate`, `resolveChoice` for the `appearance.language` setting's `system` · `en`, `textDirection`, the mirror `bisa.locale` read by `index.html` before the first paint), `format.mjs` (numbers, percentages, bytes, dates, `dayLabel`, `duration`, `durationPrecise`, `relative`, `ago` — `Intl` in the locale's shape, the words from `locales/en/desktop/format.ftl`), `catalog.ts` (every shipped language's `.ftl` files bundled as text), `boot.ts` (`main.tsx`'s first import: the stored or negotiated language installed before any other module says a word, the document stamped), `localeStore.ts` (`setLocale` mirrors a new tag and opens the window again once; `syncLocaleFromNode` follows the setting), `testing.mjs` + `preload.mjs` (English from `locales/en` on disk for `node --test`; `npm test` and `scripts/test desktop` preload it), `ratchetModel.mjs` + `ratchet.mjs` (the guard's scanner and the baseline writer, `ratchet.baseline.json`, `--list` printing each finding as `path:line: literal`; the scanner reads every source off the TypeScript parser's tree — `jsxProse`: a JSX text node with a letter in it whatever punctuation it holds and whatever expression stands beside it, a literal an expression child renders (`{n === 1 ? "agent" : "agents"}`), a sentence-shaped prop (`isProseProp`) — and every string or template that reads as a sentence (`isProse`: two words, or one word beside a placeable — `${n} more`), a template nested in a placeable on its own; a literal is excused by **where it stands** (an identifier prop — `isIdentifierProp` — a class helper's argument, a log line) or by a **mark** on its line or on a comment line just above (`MARKS`: *for the machine* · *for the agent* · *for the log* · *content, never translated*), never by a `className` that happens to share its line; `ratchetModel.test.mjs`) |
| `theme/` | `tokens.css` (the role contract — seventeen colour roles, two radii, and the four **material** roles `--shadow-raised`, `--shadow-floating`, `--material-blur`, `--material-saturate`, with Tailwind's `shadow-sm`/`shadow-lg`/`shadow-xl` mapped onto the two shadows so elevation is the theme's — and the type scale, a 14px body at scale 1), `themes/harbor.css` · `themes/orchard.css` · `themes/dune.css` · `themes/glass.css` · `themes/suede.css` (five families, a light and a dark side each; Glass, the default, owns bare `:root` and the *System* media block and carries alpha in `bg`, `surface`, `surface-2` and `border` and answers a blur; Suede is matte — `--shadow-raised: none`, one soft floating bloom), `material.css` (the frost: `backdrop-filter` from the material roles on every `data-pane` element and the page's ground, keyed on the glass ids and nothing else; a pane kept mounted and hidden — the terminal layer — drops it with its visibility, `shell/centerSlotModel.mjs`), `accents.css`, `density.css`, `fonts.css` (the two font dials over the bundled `@fontsource` faces), `motion.css`, `flow.css` (the canvas's `--xy-*` variables answered from theme roles), `contrast.mjs` (WCAG ratios from oklch; `composite` lays a translucent value over an opaque one in linear light), and the tests `roles.test.mjs`, `themes.test.mjs` (the contrast promise over the composite, each family's material, and the three id lists), `flowTheme.test.mjs` |
| `desktop/src-tauri/src/` | `navigation.rs` (the main window's navigation policy, every frame included — the app's own origin, the empty page, an addon's bundle on the node, nothing else; the window is built in `setup` to carry it — [18](../18-addons.md)), `edit_menu.rs` (ide/15 — the Edit menu's Cut · Copy · Paste · Select All as the shell's own items with the OS's key equivalents: a verb fired runs natively down the responder chain and is told to the webview as `edit:verb`, so a field pastes as before and a Files tree's chord fires; the menu is built on macOS alone), `browser.rs` (ide/18 — the tabs' child webviews, Tauri's `unstable` feature, standing in the browser layer `BisaBrowserLayer` that cuts a hole for each floating overlay of the main page — `mod clear`, `mod layer`, `browser_clear` — and `browser_screenshot`: `WKWebView`'s own snapshot of a tab through `objc2-web-kit`, scaled to a bounded width and encoded as PNG by AppKit, answered as raw bytes, never the screen, and refused at once for a hidden view — `SNAPSHOT_HIDDEN`, before WebKit is asked, since a snapshot after screen updates never comes from one: `browser_open` with the kit's `browserScript(relay, theme)` as the init script and http(s) navigations alone, `browser_navigate`, `browser_back`, `browser_forward`, `browser_reload`, `browser_stop` (a finish said on the page the tab is on, since the webview reports no stop), the page's title and a window it asks for said as `browser:titled` and `browser:new-window` (the window refused; the desktop opens a tab), `browser_bounds` — one atomic placement anchored on the main webview's frame and the page's viewport (`mod anchor`: the zoom, the inset, one `setFrame` on the main thread), the frame read back as `Placed` through the same anchor, the anchor logged once per change — `browser_close`, `browser_drive`, and `door::PageDoor` — a `WKScriptMessageHandler` named `bisa` on every tab's webview, the page's one door out on any origin, its words bounded (`message_of`, one JSON object under `MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES`) and re-emitted as `browser:message`; `browser:navigated` on every load with the webview's own `canGoBack`/`canGoForward`; a tab opens hidden on `about:blank` or a page and shows on its first placement; the tabs' webviews stand in no capability, so no page has IPC; every webview closed with the window), `terminal.rs`'s `terminal_open` with `run: true` (`run_launch`: the node's run command for the checkout, refused unapproved, `sh -c` through the login shell) and `terminal_cwd` (where a shell stands: `cwd_of`, its process's current directory through `sysinfo` with one pid refreshed, else the directory the session kept from its spawn — `place_of`), `sync.rs` (`Locked` — the shell's one way to take a `std::sync::Mutex`; the shell denies `let_underscore_must_use` as the crates do, the four command modules `network`, `ports`, `stats` and `terminal` allowing it only for the compile-time check `#[tauri::command]` emits beside a borrowing async command — a poisoned guard taken as it stands since the panic that poisoned it was contained and said; the mirror of `bisa_core::sync`, which the shell may not depend on; every registry — the terminals, the browser views, the sidecar, the stats table, the tray — locks through it), `main.rs` (the commands — `loose.rs`'s `read_loose_file`, `write_loose_file` and `dropped_paths` for a **loose file** (ide/03 §Loose files): a file anywhere on this machine read and saved by absolute path with the node's editor rules byte for byte — the caps, the sha256 hash, compare-and-swap, an atomic write — and, on macOS, the drag pasteboard's file URLs read after the DOM's drop so a dropped file has its path; `pasteboard.rs`'s `pasteboard_holds`, `paste_into`, `pasteboard_image`, `paste_image_into` and `paste_image_to_temp` for what the machine's clipboard holds, pasted into the explorer, a conversation or a terminal (ide/03 §The explorer, ide/06): the general pasteboard's file URLs read the same way and the files copied in this process into a folder of a checkout by absolute path — a taken name Finder's *name 2*, a folder whole, a symlink, a nested `.git` and a folder into itself left out and named in the `PasteReport` — and its picture as PNG bytes (its PNG as it is, else its TIFF encoded by `png.rs`, the one encoder the browser's snapshot shares) handed to the webview for an attachment, or written under the name the person confirmed (`write_png`: a taken or improper name refused in a sentence) — the node's watcher announcing what landed — or, for a terminal, written to a file of its own in the machine's temporary folder (`bisa-pasted-pictures`, `write_png_free`: the folder made when missing, a taken name Finder's *name 2*) whose path the terminal types; `ports::listening_ports` and `ports::stop_port` scan the socket table and `SIGTERM` a port's process; `attribution.rs` is the one walk both machine readers share — `Roots` (this process, the node, each terminal's shell, each harness session by pid) and `chain(pid)`, every root met on a pid's parent chain, the port scan taking the terminal wherever it stands and the resource read the nearest; `stats::host_info` reads what the machine is once and `stats::resource_usage` its load and every named process's share — CPU, memory, bytes read and written over the interval — from a process table kept between reads (`stats::Table`), so the deltas span the footer's own cadence; `gpu.rs` reads the accelerator's load on macOS through `/usr/sbin/ioreg`, under a two-second budget; `probe.rs` is the one way the shell runs a program for its output — stdout drained on a thread, `try_wait` to the deadline, ended past it — shared by the login shell's `PATH`, `ioreg` and the network readers; `run_or_defer` — the one variant that lets a late program finish in the background up to a cap and hands its answer to a closure, for the probe whose late answer is worth keeping for next time; `network.rs` reads this Mac's network for the footer's network word and Settings › Capabilities › Network through the OS's own unprivileged readers, each parsed by a pure function a fixture holds — `scutil --nc list` (the VPN services System Settings knows and whether each is connected), `ifconfig -a` (every interface but the loopback with its addresses — `parse_interfaces`; up is `UP` and `RUNNING` with no `status: inactive` and an address that is not link-local, so macOS's own `utun`s and a port whose cable is out are not up; the tunnel families are the VPN's), `networksetup -listallhardwareports` (which device is the Wi-Fi and which an Ethernet — `kind_of`), `netstat -rn -f inet` (the default route, the first `default` row that is not interface-scoped, and each interface's routes and gateway), `scutil --dns` (the resolvers, the machine's and the ones bound to an interface), `scutil --proxy` (what System Settings names) — and the one probe that leaves the machine, `read_internet`: two `TcpStream::connect_timeout`s to well-known addresses on 443 and one `GET` through `curl` under `probe::run` to the echo service handed in (`--proxy` from the pair System Settings names, never a PAC; the body must be one address, `parse_echo`), the verdict `internet(tcp, echo)` — up on a connect or an echoed address, the cause worded from curl's exit code; the proxy read first and the rest under `std::thread::scope` at once, so the whole read is about one budget — joined by `join` into `NetworkFacts { internet, interfaces, vpn, default_route, dns, proxy, read_ms }` with the provider read off the process table from one `PROVIDERS` table; `network_facts(public_ip_url)` answers it or `null` where there is no reader; a read, never a write; `disk::data_dir_usage` walks the data dir once into sizes at depth one and two, the index's three files one entry, and names the volume it lives on; `copy_text` and `copy_image` put text or a PNG on the clipboard through the shell — the webview writes its own only inside a user gesture, a menu's chosen action runs after the menu has left and a screenshot lands seconds after its click, so every copy in the desktop goes through this door (`ui/clipboard.ts`, `copyText` · `copyImage`); `set_window_appearance` puts the title bar on the page's side and the family's material behind the page — the OS's under-window effect (`UnderWindowBackground` on macOS, `Mica` on Windows) for a glass family, `None` for an opaque one; the window is `transparent` with `macOSPrivateApi` on, and an opaque family's page hides that entirely; `shutdown_all` on window destroy and on exit), `sidecar.rs` (`BISA_API_BASE` or spawn `bisa node --listen 127.0.0.1:<free port>` with a minted `BISA_API_TOKEN`; `BISA_BIN` → the node the bundle carries beside the executable (`scripts/macos/lib.sh` puts it in `Contents/MacOS/`, a universal Mach-O signed with the hardened runtime before the app is) → `PATH` → `../target/debug/bisa`; `wait_for_health`), `terminal.rs` (one PTY per tab via `portable-pty`; the scope and id resolve to a directory through `GET /placement/{scope}/{id}`, the harness id to a program through `GET /harnesses` output over an ordered `tauri::ipc::Channel`; the **process watcher** — every two seconds while a shell is live, the process table read once and each PTY's descendants walked for a program the catalog names, `harness_of` / `harness_under`, a change one `Process` event on the tab's own channel), `login_env.rs` (the `PATH` a person actually has — the login shell asked once per process under a four-second budget, its answer remembered in the app's config folder (`remember_in`, `app_config_dir()/login_path`) and used when the shell is late, the late shell left to finish in the background and refresh the memo for the next launch; a miss is a line in the log, never silent), `logging.rs` (the desktop's diagnostic log — `bisa-log` by path, installed before anything else and attached **before the node exists**: `attach_from_binary` asks the `bisa` binary where the workspace is (`bisa paths --json`), so the shell never spells a workspace path; `attach_from_node` attaches again from `GET /workspace`'s `logs_dir` when nothing is writing yet — the first launch, the workspace made by the node itself — and after every restart; `attach_fallback`, in `setup`, the app's own log folder when no binary named a workspace, so a bundled app that cannot find its node is never silent; the recorder's replay carries what was said before into the first file; `RunEvent::Exit` says goodbye; two commands — `log_event`, the webview's one door, a level word, a surface, a message and its fields, bounded again here; `log_configure`, the machine's `logging.*` as the webview read them; the node's start failure, a restart and the desktop's own start are lines of it) The window's `dragDropEnabled` is off (`tauri.conf.json`): the webview takes no OS file drops of its own, so the page's own HTML5 drags — the palette onto the canvas, a file within the explorer — reach the DOM; `permissions.rs` (the two macOS grants Settings › Capabilities › System reads and asks for — `system_permission_status` never prompts: Full Disk Access is a read-only open of TCC's own database with the handle dropped unread, the microphone `AVCaptureDevice.authorizationStatus`; `system_permission_request` lets macOS ask once for the microphone, with the usage string `Info.plist` carries — refused as `unsupported` when it is absent, since macOS would terminate a process that asks without one — and opens the System Settings pane by argv for Full Disk Access, which has no prompt; every other platform answers `unsupported` with the reason, the first `target_os` cfg in the crate); `../icons/` is the OS icon set `bundle.icon` names, rasterised by `just app-icon` from `logo/logo.svg` — never drawn by hand `sidecar.rs` supervises the node it spawned: `NodeState::supervise` is a watchdog thread reading the child once a second, the child's stderr piped to one reader thread keeping its last 64 lines (`StderrTail` — what an abort prints outside the log: *thread 'main' has overflowed its stack*, an allocation failure, a loader's refusal — each line at `debug` under `bisa_desktop::node`), and an exit the shell did not ask for is an `error` line with its pid, exit code or signal and the tail, a crash report of kind `child_exit` through the shell's handle, and a respawn on the **same port** after `restart_delay(attempt)` — 1 s doubling to 30 s, reset after a minute of health (a pure function with a unit test) — every respawn, and a person's *Restart*, reaching the webview as the `node:restarted` event (`NodeRestarted { port, attempt, requested }`) ; `export_file` (a rendering's SVG or PNG saved where the person picks), `restart_node` (Settings › Node), `node_status` (the node as the shell supervises it — running, pid, external, restarts, healthy for how long — for the footer's node overlay), `artifacts::open_artifact` and `artifacts::copy_artifact`; `terminal_write`, like `terminal_open` and `terminal_close`, is `async` over `spawn_blocking`, so a child that stopped draining its PTY never holds the window's main thread, and the registry's lock recovers from a poison; `tray/` — the menu bar icon the app lives in ([The menu bar icon](../../guide/the-desktop.md#the-menu-bar-icon)): `mod.rs` (`install` in `setup`, held as state — the menu's five lines as `TrayItem` ids `tray:status` · `tray:needs` · `tray:open` · `tray:dock` · `tray:quit`; a left click opens the window, the menu is the right click's; `present(report)` relabels the two live lines, sets the title to the count while owed, the tooltip and the icon; `main_window` — the one lookup, `get_window(MAIN_WINDOW)`, by the window's label: a *webview window* is a window whose only webview is its own, and `main` carries the IDE's browser tabs as child webviews (`browser::label_of`), so `get_webview_window` would find nothing with a tab open — a guard in `scenarios/tray.test.mjs` keeps that call out of the shell; `show_window` reveals the app then shows, un-minimises and focuses `main`; `hide_window` puts the app away through `Platform::conceal`; the needs line opens the Inbox through `tray:go`; *Quit Bisa* shows the window and emits `bisa:quit-requested`, so the webview's one flow asks and saves; *Show in Dock* applies at once and is told to the webview as `tray:dock`), `report.rs` (`TrayReport` and `TrayState` as the webview sends them — the words are the webview's; a state is a dot in the OS's colours or none for calm; the title and the tooltip), `glyph.rs` (the shipped mark `icons/tray/36x36.png` — `just tray-icon` over `logo/tray-mark.svg`, 18 pt at 2× — tinted to the menu bar's ink and the dot painted at the lower right with a clear ring, pure functions over bytes), `platform.rs` (the seam for other OSes: `Platform` — `has_dock`, `set_dock_visible`, `conceal` and `reveal` as a pair — on macOS the app hides as ⌘H does and un-hides, so ⌘Tab and the Dock bring it back as the OS makes them; elsewhere the window hides and shows — `menu_bar_ink`; `MacOs` under the one cfg, `Elsewhere` answering *unsupported*; the ink is the **system's** appearance, `AppleInterfaceStyle` in the global defaults, never the window's theme — the page sets that to its own scheme and tao applies it to the whole app — re-read at every repaint, when the window is focused or shown and when its theme moves); the two held doors in `main.rs`: `on_window_event` holds `CloseRequested` on `main` and emits `bisa:close-requested`, `ExitRequested { code: None }` is held and emits `bisa:quit-requested`, `Reopen` with no visible window shows it; the commands `hide_window`, `tray_report` and `tray_dock`; `words.rs` (`ShellWords` — the menu bar icon's fixed lines and the Edit menu's verbs in the webview's language, set by the `shell_words` command; English until the webview has spoken), `window_state.rs` (the window opens where it was left: its normal bounds — the outer corner in physical pixels with the scale it was reported at, the inner size in logical pixels — and whether it stood maximized or fullscreen, in `window.json` under `app.path().app_config_dir()` as `{ "v": 1, "bounds": { … } }`, written beside then renamed, only when it changed, another version or a file that does not parse refused for the default; `WindowState::load` and `placement` before the window is built in `setup`, `note` at every `Moved`, `Resized` and `ScaleFactorChanged` of `main`, `keep` at a held close, in `hide_window`, at `Focused(false)` and first at `Exit`; the rules take plain numbers — `fit(saved, screens, space, minimum, default)` keeps a place only where the start of the title bar lands on a screen there is now, looked for in the `Space` the OS lays its screens out in (logical pixels on macOS, where a physical number is the logical one times the scale of the window's screen; physical elsewhere), and holds the size inside that screen's work area, above the minimum; `Track` keeps the bounds from before a run of changes, so an animated zoom — whose frames say *normal* until the last says *zoomed* — leaves the bounds the window comes back to; the window is handed in as a `Window`, never looked up as a webview window) |

### Mount order

`ToastProvider` → `WorkspaceContext` (`useWorkspaceState()`: goals, channels, DMs, agents, projects,
workstreams, inbox, unread — loaded once, patched by one bus subscription) → `TopChrome` →
`Sidebar` → the routed `Screen` inside an `ErrorBoundary` and `Suspense` (its fallback a `Spinner` in place, shown only past the kit's beat; every screen's chunk is preloaded at idle after the first paint — `SCREENS`, `preloadScreens` — so navigation after boot never suspends) → `AuxPane` → … — every host the screen's boundary cannot reach in a boundary of its own: the top chrome, the sidebar, the details pane and the status bar each in an `ErrorBoundary` with a one-line fallback in place, and every overlay mounted once in the shell — the who-commits dialog, the close confirmation, the palette, the notes panel, the companion, the two toast rails — in an `OverlayBoundary` (closed, one toast saying so, back on the next route), so a throw in one costs that host and never the window; the shell's own `ErrorBoundary` in `main.tsx` is the last line, a card with *Reload* and a line in the log; and a shell whose node did not start still opens — `api_base` answers the reason, the offline line says it (`workspaceLoadModel.offlineWords`), and the sidecar's watchdog keeps trying on the crash curve until `node:restarted` brings the base back;
`TerminalPanel` (outside the routed screen, so a shell survives navigation; drawn `position:fixed`
over the rect the workbench's centre publishes to `layerSlots.ts`) → `Omnibox` → `NoteOverlay` →
`PetOverlay` → `AddonLayer` (the addon windows, [18](../18-addons.md), in their own boundary at the pet's tier).

### Routing

`router.ts` is a typed hash router with no dependency, its facts in `routeModel.mjs` (the table,
`parse(hash, home)` ⇄ `href` as inverses for every route — the **home** is the caller's, the route an
empty, bare or unknown hash lands on, because the model knows no person; `section`, `applySearchPatch`)
and the window and the hooks in the `.ts`: `useRoute` and the one plain read `currentRoute()` both hand
`parse` the home `navOrderStore.homeRoute()` says — the first destination of the person's sidebar
order — so a launch on a machine that remembers no place, whose window has no hash, opens where the
person put the first row, the Inbox until something is above it; `indexRouteOf(route)` is the list of
a route's section, the Inbox for a section that has none. `Route` is `pulse | inbox | goals | goal { id } |
workflows | workflow { id } | run { id } | projects | workbench { scope, id } | channels | channel { id } | messages |
dm { id } | hosted_channel { host, id } | hosted_dm { host, id } | conversation { id } | agents | agent { id } | teams | settings`; `WorkbenchScope = Exclude<FileScope, "run">` — the generated
type but a run in the workspace's folder, which the IDE reaches through the run's work items; a
terminal's scope, a link's root and a browser tab's home are the same list (`TerminalScope`, the
`FileScope` of `ui/linkModel.d.mts`, `BrowserScope`), so the backend's list of scopes stays the only
list. `#/workflows` is the library and `#/workflows/:id` the
designer; a run in the workspace has a page of its own, `#/runs/:id`, under the Workflows section; a
goal's run is drawn on the same canvas but lives on the goal (`#/goals/:id?tab=workflow&run=`), and
its id opened at `#/runs/` is handed on there.
`#/projects` lands in the IDE (`ideHomeModel.homeRoot`: a home is an open workstream of a listed
project — the remembered root while it is one, else the first project's primary by name, else the
empty landing; an archived project's checkouts are nobody's). The same model says where the IDE goes
when the project under the person is archived or removed (`homeAfterLeaving`, `leavesRoot`,
`homeRoute`). `useRoute` and `useSearchParams` are memoised on the
hash on purpose: a fresh object per render once produced an infinite effect loop.

**Every address is resolved against where the person was** (`shell/placeMemoryModel.mjs`, kept by
`shell/placeMemoryStore.ts` under `bisa.view.places`). A **place** is what the address says — a path
and the query that is the screen's state; the memory is `{ v, owner, last, sections, queries }`: the
last place stood on, each section's, and the query each path was left with (64 paths, 512 characters
a query).

- **Which keys are a place is a table.** `REMEMBERED` names, per route, the query keys that are the
  screen's state; `PANE_KEYS` is the Details pane's group (`aux`, `auxId`, `stage`, `insp`);
  `ONE_SHOT` is what a link hands over once (`panel`, `run`, `step`, `edit`, and `join` — an
  invitation code, which never reaches the memory). A key no table names is dropped from the memory,
  and a guard in the model's test fails when a key the desktop reads with `useSearchValue` or writes
  with `setSearch` is in no table.
- **The router resolves before React reads.** `installRouter()` runs in `main.tsx` before the first
  render; the resolved hash is a module variable the one `hashchange` listener moves, and
  `useAddress` reads that through `useSyncExternalStore` — never `window.location.hash` — so no
  render sees an address that was not resolved. A launch with no hash stands on `launchPlace()`.
- **Arrival or exact, by who wrote the entry.** An entry the router resolved is stamped in
  `history.state`. A stamped entry — written by `setSearch`, or reached by Back or Forward — is
  **exact**: taken as it is, and recorded. An unstamped one — a link, `navigate`, `replace` — is an
  **arrival**: with no state keys it is given the query its path remembers, its one-shot keys kept;
  with state keys they stand, and the pane's group is filled from the memory when the link names
  none; then the entry is stamped by `replaceState`, which adds no entry and raises no event. So a
  bare link onto a filtered list keeps the filters, a filter cleared by hand stays cleared, and Back
  onto an old entry never rewrites it. An arrival is written to storage at once; a change in place
  waits 250 ms, since a filter box writes an entry a keystroke.
- **A section's door** — the sidebar's row, the rail's icon, the palette's *Go to* — reads
  `sectionHash(places, section, current)` through `shell/sectionDoor.ts` (`useSectionHref`,
  `goToSection`): from another section, the place the person left; from inside one of the section's
  details, its list with the query the list was left with; from its list, nowhere. Projects has no
  list: its door is the IDE's last root.
- **A screen asks whether the person had been on it.** `placeWasKnown(path)` is what the arrival
  learned — the goal page opens its Details pane only for a goal never been on.
- **What is gone is forgotten.** `shell/gonePlacesModel.mjs` names the paths a fact on the bus takes
  out (`goal_deleted`, `workflow_deleted`, `project_deleted`, a conversation deleted, a workstream
  closed) and the hosted workspaces a person is no longer in (`leftHosts`, asked only once the node
  has answered its hosts); `App.tsx` hears them once and calls `forgetPlaceMemory`. A detail read
  that answers *not found* goes through `shell/useGonePlace.ts` — a goal's, a workflow's, a run's, a
  channel's, a direct channel's and a conversation's page each hand it their read: the place is
  forgotten, and a person who had been on it is taken to the section's list with one toast — one who
  followed a link keeps the screen's *not found* note. An archived thing is still there, and so is its place.

### The designer

`@xyflow/react` (pinned, MIT; `zustand`, `classcat` and `@xyflow/system` come with it) is imported
in `ui/FlowCanvas.tsx` and nowhere else — `ui/imports.test.mjs` bans `@xyflow/` outside the kit —
and reached through `ui/flow.ts`, the kit's second entry, which `ui/index.ts` does not re-export so
the library stays out of the main chunk; only `views/_workflow/` may import it. The wrapper is a
controlled graph with a **live mirror**: every change the library reports (a drag, a selection, a
box-select) is applied to the mirror, so a dragged node follows the pointer and an edge is really
selectable, and the mirror is resynced from the props whenever the caller changes the picture — a
move the caller wrote down comes back as new props and stays, one it refused leaves the props as
they were and the same resync puts the node back. It takes nodes, edges with a tone, node
types, and callbacks for the moves of one gesture (`onMoved`, every node a multi-selection dragged),
connect, select, delete and drop; it owns the keyboard on the focused
canvas (`Delete`/`Backspace`, `Mod+D`, `Mod+Z`, `Mod+Shift+Z`, `Escape`) and imports the library's
stylesheet inside its chunk. The palette drops a step onto the canvas by HTML5 drag and drop under
`STEP_MIME`, and it lands where it was dropped. One node type, `StepNode`: one target
handle on top and `loop-in` on the right (an anchor a loop arrives at, never a drop target); `out` at
the bottom — one per branch for a branching kind (a `decide`'s rules plus `otherwise`, an `if`'s
`yes`/`no`, a `switch`'s cases plus `otherwise`, a loop's `each`/`loop` and `done`), each with the
id `branch:<name>` so a branch may be named anything — `fail` on the left and `loop-out` on the
right, both anchors a route is drawn from and never dragged out of; badges for `join: any`,
`on_fail ≠ fail`, `max_visits ≠ 3` and the problem count; a run mode ring and a `StepStateChip`.
Positions come from `workflowLayout.mjs`: a step's own (`Step.position`) when it has one, else the
layered layout's at the estimated sizes (`layout` → `placeMissing`), and every canvas edit goes
through `Designer.commit`, which writes the derived ones down (`withPositions`); `tidy` lays every
card out again at the measured sizes and sorts `steps` into reading order; `snapTo` and `nudgeFree`
put a dropped card on the grid and beside — never under — a card it would cover; every flow is a
smoothstep from its handle to the other's, a loop or an on-fail from its side handle, every one
with an arrowhead; the canvas reports each card's measured size back (`onMeasured`) and brings a
just-added step into view (`reveal`, one counter the screen owns; a drop or a duplicate on the canvas
asks through `onReveal`). It opens where the caller says — `startViewport`, read once at mount, the
first fit skipped — else fitted to the picture, and reports where it looks after every pan or zoom
(`onViewport`, xyflow's `onMoveEnd`); the library's designer keeps both, with the step picked, per
workflow (`designerMemoryStore.ts`, over the view memory — so across a restart too), so a designer
opened again by any door lands where it was left, and a remembered step is no new pick — the pane
the person left open stays. The goal's Workflow tab keeps its own, under the goal's place. The resync from the props is **content-addressed** (`ui/flowMirrorModel.mjs`): a caller that re-renders with the same picture commits nothing, and a real change carries xyflow's measurement across, so no frame costs a re-measure.

The document is a `designerSession`: validation follows the cursor (`useLiveValidation`, 200 ms; a
validation that could not run is an `error` the problems list shows above the node's last word), a
save is the head of the history at the base revision when the session says it is due
(`saveDue`: `workflow.autosave.delay_ms` of quiet, or ten delays after the first unsaved edit,
whichever first; `useAutosave` keeps the clock, one save in flight at a time), and when it lands
the base moves while the history stays — a keystroke typed during the save survives and undo
never resends a stale revision. Every outcome enters the session through `settle`. The designer's
own `workflow_changed` is ignored; a newer revision from elsewhere is adopted when clean and is a
conflict banner with two exits when dirty. A failed save re-arms with a capped backoff; a body the
node could not read (a 400 with no `problems`) is `rejected` and never resent — an edit is; the
screen is keyed by its route and leaving flushes with `keepalive` after the save in flight; while
dirty it is a dirty source of `editorRegistry`, so the quit question names it and the close flow
saves it. *New workflow* creates an empty draft on the node before the designer opens, so every save is an update and the Agent pane is never muted. Nothing the
designer births carries a blank reference: `blankStep`, the forms and the condition editor write
`null` for a choice not made, which the node lists as `unfilled` where `""` is a body it refuses.

### Stores

Module stores over `useSyncExternalStore`; a `snapshot()` returns raw state, never a freshly built
object.

| Store | Owns |
|---|---|
| `shell/useTerminals.ts` | terminal tabs and the pane tree; open, reveal, focus, close, restart, split, move, ratio, liveness, and the roster session a reported harness is (`sessionId`), and `running` — the harness the shell's process watcher reads under a plain shell, `harnessOf` the one word every surface reads for what a tab is about; closing a tab is what terminates a harness, `watchAborts` closes the tab on an aborted frame whatever its liveness, and `closeTerminalsRootedAt(workstreams, goal?)` closes every tab rooted at a workstream — and at the goal, when one is named — without asking, as one move (`closeTerminalTabs` → `terminalsModel.closeTerminals`: one store update for N unmounts); the retirements' verb and every workstream close's (`closeWorkstream.ts`); `terminalTyped(key, generation, data)` — the person typed into a tab: when the roster says the harness in it, or one of its sub-agents, waits and the keystroke answers a dialog (`terminalsModel.answerDue`), the node is told once per wait through the shell's `terminal_answered` and the session's own `answered` door, since no hook of the harness's says how a dialog was answered; `watchAborts` gathers the `aborted` rows of one tick and closes their tabs the same way |
| `shell/layerSlots.ts` · `shell/LayerSlot.tsx` | the rect each native-layer host publishes — the workbench's centre, the Details pane's Browser occupant — with which layer and which tab stands there; notifies only on change |
| `shell/BrowserPane.tsx` · `shell/BrowserBar.tsx` · `shell/BrowserDoor.tsx` · `shell/browserDoors.ts` | the Details pane's `browser` occupant (ide/18) and the browser's doors — `showBrowserTab(key)` shows a tab where `revealPlan` says over the published centre (the strip at home with documents in the centre, else the pane), `openBrowserAt(home, url?)` opens a tab at a home and shows it (the Browser button's, the agents' bridge's), `useNewBrowserHereDoor` answers the keymap's `NEW_BROWSER_HERE`, `openBrowserPane`, `openUrlInBrowser`, `openArtifactInBrowser` — : every tab in a strip, the shared bar whole — back, forward, reload or Stop, the address, the wand, the served chip, the camera's two buttons — *Copy a screenshot* through the shell's clipboard, *Save a screenshot as…* — never a menu over the page they photograph, both held with the reason while a shot is taken, *Open in the Project IDE*, the machine's browser, each held with its reason when the tab lacks it, answering the browser chords for the host that draws the tab — over the `aux` host's slot, the note box and the tray under the page (the checkout's `CheckoutAnnotationTray`, else the screen's); `BrowserDoor.tsx`, a screen's Browser button over `browserDoorModel.mjs`, is mounted on no screen: the Browser button is the Project IDE's alone (`BrowserLauncher.tsx`), and beside any other screen the pane opens by ⌘⇧L or the palette; the live dot an agent's browsing lights is `browserActivityStore.ts` over `browserActivityModel.mjs`, fed by the bridge's `working`, read by the footer; the doors the footer, the palette and ⌘⇧L come through — the pane on a tab, a URL where the person is, an artifact on an origin of its own; `auxPaneModel.toggledAux` the toggle that closes at once whatever tab the pane shows |
| `shell/auxPaneModel.mjs` | the Details pane's toggle (`toggledAux`) and its width: `auxBounds(available)` — a floor of `AUX_MIN_WIDTH` and a ceiling of `AUX_MAX_SHARE` (seven tenths) of the content column `App.tsx` measures, never below the floor, unbounded above until measured — and `shownWidth(stored, bounds)`, the width drawn for the one chosen (`AUX_WIDTH_KEY`), the choice itself never rewritten by a window resize; `AuxPane.tsx` spells no number of its own |
| `shell/auxSlot.ts` · `shell/auxSlotModel.mjs` | the Details pane's slot as one published fact: the pane hands its element over through a callback ref as it mounts the slot and `null` as it goes, `AuxPortal` follows through `useAuxSlot` — never a lookup by id, which captured a closed pane's element and reopened it empty; `createSlot` is the rule (the same element twice is silent). In one column the pane takes the width and the screen is **hidden, never unmounted**, so the screen that fills the pane is still there to fill it |
| `shell/sessionsStore.ts` · `shell/sessionRosterModel.mjs` | the roster: seeded from `GET /sessions`, reseeded on the bus's closed→open edge (a node restart), moved by `session_state` / `session_gone`, a 60 s safety refetch that pauses while hidden; commits only a snapshot that differs (`snapshotEqual.mjs`), so an unchanged frame re-renders nothing; **never an older word** — every frame carries the row's `revision`, a frame older than the row held moves nothing (`upserted` returns the same array: no commit, no transition announced), the frames heard while a read is out keep the newest word of each row (`latest`) and a whole read lands beside them by revision (`landedRead`: a read that answered after a frame wins, one from before does not, a row said gone stays gone) |
| `shell/useSettledSessions.ts` | the roster as the tabs say it, live while either store moves (`terminalsModel.settledRoster` over `useSessions` and `useTerminals`): the one roster the pet, the tray, the Agents screen and an addon's summary read, so a tab that exited settles its mark, pill, count and footer alike; its own module because the terminals store imports the sessions store (a hook there importing back would be a cycle) |
| `ui/MoreMenu.tsx` | the `⋮` — one square and the kit `Menu` behind it, drawn once for every row that keeps its verbs behind a menu: the Changes tree, a branch, a tag, a remote and its branches — every row with a `⋮` |
| `ui/find/` | find, and find and replace, as one fact and one bar: `findModel.mjs` (`Find { query, regex, caseSensitive, replacement }`, `compileFind` — an invalid expression is `null`, never a throw — `matchesOf`, `replaceAll`, `replaceOne`, `stepIndex`, `countWords`), `FindBar.tsx` (the query, `i/n`, `.*`, `Aa`, ↑ ↓, ×, the replacement field with *Replace* and *Replace all* when the surface takes one), `useDomFind.ts` over `domFindModel.mjs` (`segmentsOf`, `locate` — a match across two text nodes is two spans) drawing with the CSS Custom Highlight API; the terminal's bar, a rendering's bar and the sheet's cell search are all this — a chord in `shell/shortcuts.ts` (`find`, `replace`, `when: document`) |
| `shell/useWorkspaceData.ts` · `shell/workspaceLoadModel.mjs` | the workspace index every screen reads — goals by status, the hosted memberships with the channels each reaches, and a reload on `workflow_proposed`; the reads of a load settle **each on its own** (`settleLoads`): a route that answered is applied, one that did not keeps its last value and is named in `degraded`, which the top chrome shows as *n lists could not be read* (`degradedWords`); *offline* is the node itself being unreachable, nothing else. One 500 — a goals list that could not read a run — used to empty every list and put the *node unreachable* banner up on every run frame ; `reloadOnReconnect(watch, reload)` reads the bus's closed→open edge as one gap — a `connecting` in between included — and reloads every list once with the toast `reconnectWords()` (*The node came back — everything was read again*), which is how what a restarted node resumed, failed and withdrew reaches the screen; the roster (`sessionsStore`) reseeds through the same helper; the offline line is the bus's too (`offlineLine`): *waiting for the node…* stands from the moment the stream ends, before any load found nobody |
| `shell/visibility.ts` | the one answer to "should a background timer run right now", from `document.hidden` read live at the moment of asking — never a cached answer, since a webview that reported hidden before its first paint once latched every clock off — with `visibilitychange`, `focus` and `pageshow` all heard: `isHidden` / `isAwake` / `onVisibilityChange` / `useVisible`. Every read-only poller and cosmetic clock gates on it and runs once on becoming visible; the SSE bus and autosave never do |
| `shell/clock.ts` | one shared, visibility-gated `setInterval` per cadence (`useClock(periodMs)`), so many relative times and live counters share one timer that pauses when hidden rather than one each |
| `shell/workstreamStatusStore.ts` | every workstream's `WorkstreamStatus` from one `GET /workstreams/status`: polled every 15 s while something reads it and the window is visible, re-read at once on the facts `shell/workstreamFramesModel.mjs` names (`movesStatuses`: `workstream_opened` · `workstream_changed` · `workstream_committed` · `workstream_edited` · `committer_set` · `project_changed` — the one list `useWorkstream.ts` narrows to its checkout with `movesWorkstream`, so a commit from Git › Changes and the workstream's own reach every surface alike) and once per 150 ms burst of `file_changed`, again when the bus comes back (`reloadOnReconnect`), asked again by a git act that raises no frame (`refreshWorkstreamStatuses`, the Git tab's reload); snapshots commit only when the list differs. `useWorkstreamStatuses` (the project rail's rows **and the Board's cards** — the Board polls nothing of its own), `useWorkstreamStatus(wid)` (the right panel's rail marks) |
| `shell/statsStore.ts` (over `shell/statsModel.mjs` — what a reading changes: the machine's facts read once, a reading that lands as the load, one that fails keeping the last reading marked stale and logged once per reason, the processes a change only when a row or the interval moved) / `shell/portsStore.ts` / `shell/networkStore.ts` | the footer's resources, the listening-port scan and the network read-out (one part — this Mac's facts from the shell, asked with the echo URL `network.public_ip_url` names, what the platform leaves through from the node, the window's own `online` and the read's `error` — read every `cache.desktop.network_poll_ms`, again on a `network.*` write, on focus and on the window's `online`/`offline`, `readAt` kept without a repaint; Settings › Network reads it too): awake-gated, paused when hidden, and idempotent — the stats store is three parts compared on their own, the host (`useHostLoad`, the bar's values, and what the machine is, read once), the processes (`useProcessShares`, an open overlay's rows, compared row by row with the read's cost left out) and the disk (`useDiskUsage`, on its own slow cadence), so a reading that did not move re-renders nothing |
| `shell/resourceDimensionStore.ts` | the dimension each footer overlay shows — the resources' per metric, the browser's under `browser` — the person's choice, one JSON object under `bisa.footer.resources`; `useChosenDimension`, `chooseDimension` |
| `shell/harnessUsageStore.ts` | what each harness's account has left, by harness id: read while a surface shows it (the footer's stat, the picker's rows, a folded line), polled every `USAGE_POLL_MS` while any does, paused when hidden; `refreshHarnessUsage` asks the node's source again; an answer settles through `harnessUsageModel.settled` — a failed re-read, the node's *failed* or the node unreachable alike, keeps the last report for `USAGE_KEEP_S` with the reason as `stale`; a failure with nothing kept is asked again on `harnessUsageModel.retryDelay`'s backoff (15 s, 30 s, 60 s) before the poll, and every shown line is read again when the bus comes back after the node was away (`reloadOnReconnect`) |
| `shell/footerUsageStore.ts` | the harness the footer's usage stat shows — the person's pin under `bisa.footer.usage`; `usePinnedUsage`, `pinUsage` |
| `shell/pathIndexStore.ts` | the path index per root, in an LRU of the few most-recently-used roots (`lru.mjs`) so it does not grow for the life of the session; fetched through `shell/singleFlight.mjs`, one request shared by every asker and bound to none; patched from the `file_changed` frame **whole** — `dir` and `ignored` handed to `quickOpenScore.patchIndex`, whose `listed` keeps out what the node's walk leaves out (a path the ignore rules match, a hidden name), so a build filling `target/` adds no row and a folder made whole is read again |
| `views/_workbench/workbenchStore.ts` | which documents are open per root, and the ones recently closed there (`reopenLastClosedDoc`) — deliberately not persisted |
| `views/_workbench/rightPanelStore.ts` | `Occupant = files \| git \| workstreams \| agents \| about` (from `rightPanelModel.mjs`: `RAIL_GROUPS` — Files · Git, then Workstreams · Agent · About — `OCCUPANTS`, `OCCUPANT_LABEL`, `OCCUPANT_COMMAND`, `availableOccupants`, `resolveOccupant`, `railGroups`, `pressOccupant`, `recallFor`, `parseRememberedTabs`, `parseViews`, `aboutBody`), `GitView = changes \| branches \| history \| stashes`, `AboutView = project \| checkout \| settings`; the state is `{open, tab, root, byRoot, views}` — any occupant is remembered per root, the view of each viewed occupant once (`setPanelView`, `openPanelView`); `pressOccupant` is the rail tab's rule (open · switch · close the column), `toggleRightPanel` the ⌘⌥B one; `settleOccupant` puts the column on an occupant without opening or closing it — what the workbench does when the centre answered a door for Agent (`availableOccupants` takes the centre and drops `agents` while it is the conversation; the rail draws that tab muted and its press goes through `showRightPanel` like any door) |
| `views/_workflow/designerPanelStore.ts` | the Workflow Designer's right panel — `{open, tab}`, the pane `properties`, `agent` or `runs` — under `bisa.workflow.panel.open` and `bisa.workflow.panel.tab`, once for the machine; `useDesignerPanel`, `showDesignerPane(tab)` (opens, never closes — a chord's and a link's door), `pressDesignerPane` (the rail's press), `toggleDesignerPanel`, `revealSelection(selected)` (a step picked shows Properties); the rules in `designerPanelModel.mjs` over `ui/iconRailModel.mjs` |
| `shell/placeMemoryStore.ts` | where the person was (§Routing), under `bisa.view.places`, read once when the module loads so the router may ask before anything mounts; the router is the one writer — `land(hash, exact)` — and the rest ask: `launchPlace`, `placeWasKnown(path)`, `placesNow`, `usePlaces` (what a section's door reads), `forgetPlace`, `adoptPlaceOwner`, `forgetEveryPlace`, `flushPlaces`; the rules in `placeMemoryModel.mjs` |
| `shell/viewMemoryStore.ts` | what every screen keeps of itself — three memories over one class, `KeptMemory` (`keptMemoryModel.mjs`: by place and name, bounded, versioned, one workspace's, a value read back by identity until it changes, `keepQuietly` for what is read once at mount, a subscription per place and name, written on a 400 ms beat and 2 s at most): `viewState` under `bisa.view.state` (each place's named values and its scroll — 96 places, 8 KiB a value), `docViews` under `bisa.view.docs` (what is large and a document's own: an editor's view, a document's mode, a design in progress — 128 places, 64 KiB a value) and `threadPlaces` under `bisa.view.threads` (where each thread was being read — 64 threads). A **place** is a route's path (`placeOf(route)`) or a name for what is no route — `ide:<scope>:<id>`, `git:<scope>`, `rail`, `notes`, `draw`. `useViewState(place, name, initial, parse, store?)` is `useState` that neither a navigation nor a restart can reach — the value read from the memory on every render, parsed by the screen's model (`viewValuesModel.mjs` has the common parsers), a value equal to the initial forgotten; `useViewScroll.ts` binds the kit's `useKeptScroll` to it for every scrollport marked `data-scroll-keep`. `forgetPlaceMemory(path)`, `adoptMemoryOwner(owner)` (another workspace's memory is forgotten whole), `flushMemories`, `settleMemories` (the way out's hand: flush, then what is left of a second since the last write), `forgetEveryMemory`, `sealMemories` (*Forget where I was*: once forgotten the three memories and the places take nothing more and write nothing more until the window opens again — `KeptMemory.seal`, `placeMemoryStore.sealPlaces` — so the last word a screen hands over on its way out is written back by no flush), `followWindowForMemories` (`pagehide`, the window hidden) |
| `views/_studio/threadsStore.ts` · `views/_studio/useThreadPlace.ts` | a thread's messages for the window's life — 24 threads, the newest 300 messages of each, read once when `useScopeMessages` mounts and written by it as it changes, never subscribed (`threadCacheModel.mjs`: `joinNewest`, `joinAround` for a write on a message above the newest page, `keptThread`) — and where each thread was being read, in `bisa.view.threads` under the chat's own key (`threadPlaceModel.mjs`: `placeFrom`, `parseThreadPlace`, `restoreStep`, `RESTORE_PAGES`); neither hears the bus |
| `views/_workbench/docViewStore.ts` | a document's view — the code editor's state, each marked scrollport's place, a page's scroll, the document's mode — in `bisa.view.docs` under the document's key, read once at mount and written quietly; `forgetViews`, `moveViews` and `forgetRootViews` from `workbenchStore`, whose `forgetRootMemory(key)` takes a root's own place, its Git panel's and every document's view when the root is gone for good — a workstream closed or retired, a project deleted, a fact on the bus (`App.tsx`) |
| `views/_workflow/designDraftStore.ts` | the design a goal's Workflow tab is drawing: its undo history per goal for the window's life — the newest `designDraftModel.MAX_DRAFTS` goals drawn, in an `Lru`; one past it has lost its history and nothing else — and the drawing as it stands in `bisa.view.docs` under the goal's place, read back once a goal as a history of one entry; dropped — there too — when it is saved, cancelled or a run starts |
| `views/_work/keptReadsStore.ts` | the last answer of a read, for the window's life — 96 reads, the least recently used gone first (`keptReadsModel.mjs`, `readKey(what, ...of)`); `useAsync(load, deps, { keep })` draws it at once when a screen comes back and reads again behind it (`refreshing`), and answers `missing` for a *not found*; never written to storage — after a restart the node is read |
| `views/_workflow/designerMemoryStore.ts` | what the library's designer was left on, per workflow — the step picked and the canvas's viewport — kept under the workflow's place in the view memory; `designerMemoryOf(workflow)` read once when a designer opens, `rememberSelectedStep`, `rememberCanvasViewport`; written quietly — nothing on screen follows the memory while it is written; the rules in `designerMemoryModel.mjs` |
| `views/_studio/conversationPickStore.ts` | the conversation each owner was last on — the IDE's roots, the designer's workflows, the notes, the drawings — under `bisa.conversations.pick`, the newest owners kept; `useRememberedPick(owner)` (the pick as a subscription and its stable setter), `rememberedPick`/`keepPick` for a hand-off; the rules in `conversationSurfaceModel.mjs` (`parsePicks`, `rememberPick`) |
| `views/_workbench/ideModeStore.ts` | the centre's mode per root — `project`, `agent` or `board` — `{byRoot}` under `bisa.ide.mode`, capped; `useIdeMode(root, fallback, boardEnabled)`, `setIdeMode`, `toggleIdeMode` (the cycle), `ideModeNow`; the rules in `ideModeModel.mjs` (`MODES`, `availableModes` — the Board only while its setting is on — `modeOf`, `modeFor`, `nextMode`, `rememberMode`, `parseRememberedModes`) |
| `views/_workbench/workbenchCentreStore.ts` | what the centre shows, per root — `documents`, `conversation` or `board` — published by the Workbench while it is mounted and withdrawn with it, the way `chatTargetStore.ts` publishes the conversation on screen; `publishWorkbenchCentre(root, centre)`, `workbenchCentreFor(root)` (`documents` for no Workbench on that root); read by `shell/browserDoors.showBrowserTab`, never subscribed |
| `views/_workbench/agentPaneStore.ts` | the context chips queued for the next message to Agents, **per workstream scope**, persisted as `bisa:context:<scope>` beside the composer's `bisa:draft:<scope>` |
| `views/_workbench/editorRegistry.ts` (over `editorRegistryModel.mjs` — which editor is active: a mount claims the slot only until another is focused and an unmount releases only its own claim; the one line waiting to be shown, the newer request replacing the older for this document or another, taken once; what the quit question counts) | dirty tabs and their save handles — and, as dirty sources, the workflow designer and every open note or drawing with unsaved work (`shell/documentGuard.ts` registers a panel's hold here), for the dot and the close guard; an editor registers one stable handle per key (its `save` and path read through refs), and `useDirtyEditors` hands out the same `Set` while the registry is unchanged — a registration notifies the workbench, so neither may follow a render's identities (ide/03) |
| `notes/notesStore.ts` · `draw/drawStore.ts` · `pet/petStore.ts` | the overlays' open and active state (and, for notes and drawings, whether the panel is maximized, and — kept in the view memory under the places `notes` and `draw`, so across a restart — the note or drawing that was open and the list's search; one that no longer exists opens nothing), and each dock's placement — `{h, x, v, y}`, px from the nearest edges, never the clamp the window paints — and, for notes and drawings, whether the dock shows and whether it paints its count (`dockVisible`, `countBadge`; the count itself is `noteCount.ts`'s and `drawingCount.ts`'s, never the store's or the panel's); the pet store also keeps the last pet shown this session (`lastActive`, not persisted) so `togglePet(true)` brings it back, else `petModel.defaultPet` — Moonrice |
| `addons/addonsStore.ts` | the addons as the node lists them (`GET /addons`, re-read on `addons_changed`, on a `settings_changed` naming `addons.enabled`, and when the bus comes back) and the machine's switch (`switchedOn`, the node's `addons_enabled` — the one source the layer, the footer and the panel read); a re-read keeps an addon's object when its record did not move (`sameAddon`); `setAddonEnabled` is optimistic (`withEnabled`, then the PATCH, put back on a refusal) and un-hides; `removeAddon` forgets the window, the put-away mark and `bisa.addons.storage.<id>`; this machine's furniture — whether the layer shows, which windows are put away (pruned to installed ids on every read), each window's dock and size (`windowOf(addon, slot, viewport)`, `moveAddon`, `resizeAddon`, `resetAddonPositions`); `useAddonsSync` is mounted once, by the layer |
| `shell/followedSessionStore.ts` | the chosen session per workstream — what the pet follows; session-only |
| `shell/TranscriptPane.tsx`, `shell/useSessionRow.ts` | the aux pane's `transcript` occupant (ide/09): one session's transcript from `?aux=transcript&auxId=<session id>`, its title and state from the roster (else the node by id), the tail (`views/_work/SessionTranscript.tsx`) and *Open as a tab* in the IDE |

### The wire

`api.ts` — one function per route, in section groups mirroring the node's modules (the
`// --- workflows and runs ---` group holds `workflows`, `workflow`, `createWorkflow` (→ `{workflow,
problems}`), `putWorkflow` (the definition plus the `revision` edited; `keepalive` on the way out),
`deleteWorkflow`, `validateWorkflow`, `goalRun`, `setGoalWorkflow` (`revision` with a `definition`),
`startRun`, `amendGoalWorkflow`, `answerStep`, `releaseStep`, `markStepDone`; `decide` carries
`inputs` and `step`); every
URL it builds is a documented route (`crates/bisa-node/tests/it/routes.rs`). **A body is built from
named keys.** The node refuses a body carrying a key its type does not declare, and TypeScript holds
only a fresh literal to a type: so every function that hands a body through takes the generated body
type (`TeamPatchBody` and `CloseGoalBody` are the generated `TeamPatch` and `CloseBody`, re-exported
under the names the screens use), a call
site hands it a literal of named keys or what a model built, and
`scenarios/bodiesFitTheSchema.test.mjs` holds what the models build to `api-schema.json` with a
validator written by hand (`scenarios/schemaFit.mjs`). The base and the
token are resolved once and kept — the token only once there is one (`apiModel.tokenToKeep`: the
shell mints it with the node's first start, and a start that failed answers an empty one, which a
page that kept it would send, and read `401`, for good) — and both are forgotten on
`node:restarted` (`forgetApiBase`). A `401` is the caller's error in the node's words, never the
node being down. **A request fails three ways** and a caller tells them apart (`apiModel.failureOf`):
its caller gave it up, and nothing is said; it outstayed its deadline — every `GET` has one
(`takesDeadline`, `API_READ_TIMEOUT_MS`), a read handed its caller's signal included, the bound made
by hand since the webview this app ships for has no `AbortSignal.any`; or nobody answered, which is
*offline*. An answer whose body cannot be read, and a stream that ends before it finished, are
sentences of the catalog. `bus.ts` — one
`EventSource` for the app with per-subscriber `Filter { stream, goal, scope, key }`; a `system` frame
is the bus's own, never a subscriber's — `{kind: "lagged", dropped}` says the node dropped events this
client read too slowly for, and the bus pulses its connection state `lagged` → `open` so everything
that re-reads on a reconnect (`reloadOnReconnect`) re-reads now. The connection's word is the
model's (`busModel.connAfter`): `connecting` until the first answer, `open`, and `closed` from the
moment the stream ends until it is open again — an attempt is no news, so nothing flickers between
two tones at every retry; `lagged` is connected (`connected`). **A stream that ends because the node
was asked to stop is the node going away**, never an error to show: one line at `info`, a node that
stays away at `debug` (`endLine`), the chrome's *waiting for the node…* at once
(`workspaceLoadModel.offlineLine`), the retries at a pace that slows, and everything read again
once when it is back. Every file the bus feeds reads again on that edge — through `useAsync`, which
does it for every read a screen makes, or `useReloadOnReconnect` — or is named with its reason in
`reconnectReads.test.mjs`. `types.gen.ts`
is generated from every `JsonSchema` type by `just gen-types` and never edited; `types.hand.ts`
mirrors the crates that carry no schema (store rows, engine events, harness events — `GoalStatus`
order, the core agent ids, the three gates), each type annotated with its source; `types.ts`
re-exports both.

### Keymap

`shell/keymapModel.mjs`: `PRESETS = default | vscode | vim`; 100 commands — 92 written out and the eight
`tab_1`…`tab_8` made from one line — each with a `label`, a
`when` scope (`global | workbench | designer | tabs | files | editor | document | terminal | browser`,
the narrowest live one winning) and a chord per preset — `mac`, where a Mac's hands differ, the chords it takes in their place (`resolveKeymap(…, mac)`: terminal pane focus is ⌘⌥+arrows on a Mac, Alt+arrows elsewhere); `always` marks the three palette chords that work inside a
composer; `typed` the six of a terminal's own text editing, bound on a Mac only and typed into the shell (`terminal/typedKeysModel.mjs`), never intercepted. `Enter`, `Space`, `Delete`, `Backspace`, `Tab` and the function keys may be bound bare; an
explicit `Ctrl` chord is the `Mod` chord off a Mac. `shell/shortcuts.ts` dispatches: the `files`
commands go to the focused tree through `ui/explorerStore.ts`, the `tabs` ones, the reveals, ⌘N and ⌘O to
the workbench through its doors, `import_project` and `rename` to the rail's. The kit shows chords beside menu items
through `ui/keymapHints.ts`, a lookup the shell provides on every reload — `ui/` never imports
`shell/`. The reference [`keymap.md`](../../reference/keymap.md) is generated from the model, one
table per scope (`node scripts/gen-keymap-docs.mjs`); [ide/15](../ide/15-keymap.md) lists the command
ids and a test keeps the two equal. The designer's canvas chords are not keymap commands: they act on the
focused canvas only; its four panel commands are, in the `designer` scope.

### Per-viewer state

Every localStorage key is `bisa.*` — with four per-scope families spelt with colons, the
composer's draft `bisa:draft:<scope>` (a note's is `bisa:draft:note:<id>`), the agent pane's chips `bisa:context:<scope>` and
the Git tab's commit message `bisa:git:<scope>` (`gitPanelStore.ts`), each keyed by its scope
and cleared when its text or tray empties, and the address tray's choice of addressees
`bisa:address:<scope>` (`addressModel.mjs`); the test
`localStorageKeys.test.mjs` keeps this list equal to the code: `bisa.accent`, `bisa.aux.width`, `bisa.browser.sessions.v3` (the browser's tabs in sight and who opened each, remembered across a restart — `browsersModel.mjs`), `bisa.locale` (the language's mirror, read by `index.html` before the first paint — `localeModel.mjs`), `bisa.sidebar.order` (the sidebar's rows as the person ordered them — `navOrderModel.mjs`), `bisa.board.archived`, `bisa.board.collapsed` (the Board's window furniture: the Archived toggle and the folded columns — its scope is the rail's selection, in the view memory), `bisa.collapsed.` (a prefix, per
collapsible), `bisa.density`,
`bisa.ide.agent`, `bisa.ide.serve.folder` (the folder last served in each checkout, which *From folder…* opens on — `shell/serveDoors.ts`), `bisa.ide.agents.view` (which IDE roots' Agent pane shows the list of conversations rather than the one it is on — `agentPaneViewStore.ts`), `bisa.chat.thinking` (whether the agents' thinking is open above their replies, `shown` or `hidden` — `thinkingStore.ts`), `bisa.conversations.pick` (the conversation each owner is on — an IDE root, a workflow, a note, a drawing — by owner key, thirty-two owners kept — `views/_studio/conversationPickStore.ts`), `bisa.ide.views` (the view each of Git and About is on, the Changes view's layout, tree or list, and its filter, one JSON object), `bisa.ide.rail.open`, `bisa.ide.rail.tab`,
`bisa.footer.usage` (the harness the footer's usage stat shows), `bisa.footer.resources` (the dimension each resource overlay shows, one JSON object), `bisa.ide.rail.archived` (whether the rail shows the projects put away), `bisa.ide.mode` (the centre's mode per root), `bisa.ide.rail.width`, `bisa.ide.right.open`, `bisa.ide.right.tabs`,
`bisa.ide.right.width`, `bisa.draw.open`, `bisa.draw.tab` (which drawings the Draw overlay lists), `bisa.draw.dock`, `bisa.draw.dockvisible`, `bisa.draw.badge`, `bisa.draw.maximized` (whether the Draw panel fills the content column), `bisa.draw.askwidth` (the conversation drawer's width beside the canvas), `bisa.draw.savedelay`, `bisa.draw.width`, `bisa.draw.height` ([19](../19-drawings.md)), `bisa.notes.dock`, `bisa.notes.height`,
`bisa.notes.maximized` (whether the notes panel fills the content column), `bisa.notes.askwidth` (the conversation drawer's width beside a note), `bisa.notes.open`, `bisa.notes.tab` (which notes the overlay lists), `bisa.notes.width`, `bisa.pet.active`, `bisa.pet.dock`,
`bisa.pet.size`, `bisa.addons.layer` (whether the addon layer shows), `bisa.addons.hidden` (the addons put away), `bisa.addons.window.` (followed by an addon's id: its window's `{dock, size}`), `bisa.addons.storage.` (followed by an addon's id: the addon's own store, a JSON bag of strings under 256 KiB — [18](../18-addons.md)), `bisa.sidebar.mode`, `bisa.sidebar.width`,
`bisa.terminal.launched`, `bisa.terminal.sessions.v3`, `bisa.theme`,
`bisa.type_scale`, `bisa.font_ui`, `bisa.font_mono`,
`bisa.view.places` (where the person was — the last place, each section's, the query each path was left with; §Routing), `bisa.view.state`, `bisa.view.docs`, `bisa.view.threads` (what every screen keeps of itself, a document's view and where each thread was being read — `shell/viewMemoryStore.ts`),
`bisa.workflow.panel.open`, `bisa.workflow.panel.tab` (the designer's right panel: whether its column shows, and which pane — Properties, Agent or Runs — once for the machine, `designerPanelStore.ts`), `bisa.workflow.panel.width`, `bisa.workflow.library.view` (the designer's Agent pane's pick is `bisa.conversations.pick`, every owner's; the step picked and the canvas's place are in the view memory). Keys spelt with a capital are outside the guard's pattern and are listed here by hand: `bisa.ide.lastRoot` (the workstream the IDE was last in — `lastRootStore.ts`) and the notes overlay's `bisa.notes.countBadge`, `bisa.notes.defaultView`, `bisa.notes.dockVisible` and `bisa.notes.saveDelay` (`notesStore.ts`). The six appearance keys are
a first-paint copy of the `appearance.*` settings, stamped by the inline script in `index.html`
before the stylesheet loads and overwritten from the node at boot and on every `settings_changed`. The four `bisa.view.*` memories are versioned (`v: 1`) and stamped with the workspace's owner key:
another version is refused, never reinterpreted, and another workspace's is forgotten whole. The
window's size and place are not the webview's: the shell keeps them in `window.json` under the app's
config folder (`window_state.rs`). Two more versioned shapes refuse
an older file rather than reinterpret it: the terminal sessions (`SESSIONS_VERSION = 3`) and the
per-root layout on the node (`LAYOUT_VERSION = 5`, `ide/layout/<scope>-<id>.json` — tabs, the active one, the pane tree, the pins and the strip's order; never a preview or an untitled document); the three dock placements (`bisa.notes.dock`, `bisa.draw.dock`, `bisa.pet.dock`) likewise refuse anything that is not exactly `{h, x, v, y}` — the earlier bottom-right `{x, y}` included — and take the default corner.

---

## Invariants held here

| Invariant | Held by |
|---|---|
| A screen imports UI libraries only through `../ui` — `@xyflow/` and `@dnd-kit/` included | `ui/imports.test.mjs` |
| A dialog's footer wraps and its body is the scrollport — no button is clipped or scrolled away; every kit dialog draws its footer through `DialogFooter` | `ui/dialogLayout.test.mjs` |
| No dialog footer outside the kit holds more than three buttons — several acts are a `ChoiceDialog` | `ui/dialogFooters.test.mjs` |
| Nothing under `src/` imports `happy-dom` or `jsdom` | `noHappyDom.test.mjs` |
| Every theme supplies every role `tokens.css` declares — the material roles included; every `--xy-*` variable the canvas is themed with is one the installed stylesheet reads | `theme/roles.test.mjs`, `theme/flowTheme.test.mjs` |
| The contrast promise: in every theme block, text on `bg` and `surface` ≥ 7:1, secondary text and accent ink ≥ 4.5:1, the status colours ≥ 3:1 on `bg`, `surface-2` a visible step off `bg`, `border` off `surface`, the accent ≥ 30° of hue from `warn` and `danger`, light backgrounds never pure white and dark ones never black — measured over the **composite** for a glass family (`surface` over `bg`, `bg` over white and over black); every accent overlay reads on the default family's surfaces and on frosted glass over either ground | `theme/themes.test.mjs` |
| A family's material is its word: an opaque family's surfaces carry no alpha and its blur is `0px`; a glass family's `bg`, `surface` and `surface-2` carry alpha behind a non-zero blur; text, the accents and the status colours are solid in every family; `material.css` is keyed on glass ids only and sets nothing but a backdrop and an edge | `theme/themes.test.mjs` |
| A bundled widget's class is never restyled by an app utility: the real stylesheet, compiled with Tailwind's own compiler, makes no bare utility named like a class xterm, Monaco, Excalidraw or xyflow styles — unless it means the same there (`fixed`, `hidden`, `italic`) or the widget's own rule sets its property (`inline`, `shadow`, `static`); the names `styles.css` leaves out (`visible`, `invisible`, `contents`, `container`) are each worn by a widget, never made, and spelled out where the app needs them (`[visibility:hidden]`, `[display:contents]`), and no class string uses one | `theme/widgetClasses.test.mjs` |
| A hidden layer paints nothing: the terminal layer keeps its last box hidden with `visibility`, and drops its frost with it — WebKit paints a hidden element's backdrop while anything inside it is visible — while a shown one is frosted by the material like any pane | `shell/centerSlotModel.test.mjs` |
| The theme ids agree across the stylesheets' `[data-theme]` selectors, `THEMES` in `shell/theme.ts` and the `appearance.theme` choice in the registry; the diagram's roles are ones the contract declares | `theme/themes.test.mjs`, `ui/mermaidModel.test.mjs` |
| A path opens as the editor, the editor with a rendered view, or a rendered document with no editor — by its extension alone, the modes per kind, a page with a split and on its source, the glyph and the word per mode, the remembered mode's key; the byte cap is the engine's word for word; the refusals say the size, the bound and the way out | `views/_workbench/fileDocModel.test.mjs` (reads `RAW_REFUSE_BYTES` from `crates/bisa-engine/src/ide/files.rs`) |
| The artifact kinds, the extension table and the media types mirror the Rust; an artifact's address round-trips; versions group by title newest first; a page's sandbox allows scripts alone and its policy reaches only the libraries, only when allowed, and the inspector lands after the policy and only when asked; a delimited text parses by RFC 4180 with the delimiter told from its first line; a deck's slides come in order with their text, pictures and notes | `ui/artifact/artifactModel.test.mjs`, `ui/artifact/pageDocument.test.mjs`, `ui/artifact/sheetModel.test.mjs`, `ui/artifact/pptxModel.test.mjs` |
| The inspector script names no network, storage or cookie; run in a page it answers a click with the element's path (a unique id, else the chain of tags with their position), its tag, a bounded excerpt and its text, drops its listeners when inspecting stops, says which badges lost their element, relays Escape and ignores a stranger; the app's parser takes the frame's words alone and bounds every field; the browser's program is the same core over the shell's door on every page — no IPC, inert with no door — and relays a browser chord pressed inside the page as the keymap's command, by ⌘ on a Mac and Ctrl elsewhere, the parser bounding it to the six | `ui/artifact/pageInspector.test.mjs` |
| An annotation is added with the next number, replaced in place when its element is pointed at again, removed with the numbers kept in step, refused without words; the rows' words and the lost element's; the chips in number order, the request's words and target under the edit contract; every badge carries its note; a draft is kept under its scope and its page | `views/_workbench/annotationModel.test.mjs` |
| An annotated element is one chip however often it is pointed at, labelled by its file and its own segment | `views/_workbench/contextChips.test.mjs` |
| The whole document is a pick: `html` and `body` answer as themselves, a pick carries its ancestors outermost first, a crumb in the page's own box re-picks that element; the inspector's two modes (`off` · `picking`) are one list; under either program — the frame's and the browser's — a pick opens the note box in the page with its crumbs, text and question and the caret in it, a box open outlines nothing under the pointer and swallows a click outside without picking while a click inside is the box's own, Enter or Add says the note with its element and closes the box, Escape or Never mind closes it alone and says so, Escape with no box open leaves, a crumb re-picks keeping the words typed, an annotated element opens with its note and *Change*, the box sits above or below clamped to the viewport and follows a scroll, the app's *off* closes everything and a repeated word installs nothing twice; the parser bounds a note and the ancestors and drops a malformed one, and refuses `bisa:rect` and `bisa:select` | `ui/artifact/pageInspector.test.mjs` |
| An edit handed to an agent settles on that agent's turn of that conversation moving from a live state to a settled one after the ask — never another agent's, another conversation's, a rest older than the ask, or a session that was not live before — and the words say how it ended; the live and settled words together are the session vocabulary | `views/_workbench/agentEditsModel.test.mjs` (reads `ui/sessionState.mjs`) |
| The disk holding what this editor last saved — the same hash — is no change on disk, dirty or not | `views/_workbench/editorModel.test.mjs` |
| Every path and URL in text is found with its address and never inside a redacted secret; the HTML pass marks text and inline code and leaves a block, an anchor and a placeholder alone; a path is named under the longest root, read from where the surface stands, looked up in each root's index, found by its tail, left to the node as an unlisted guess, or refused; the grammar climbs as far as it says and reads a hidden folder's path; a long message is scanned inside a frame | `ui/linkModel.test.mjs` |
| The appearance panel's facts: the specimen's pixels are the stylesheet's steps times the scale, a sample stamps what the DOM would carry, the cards are System first with the default family's halves | `views/_settings/appearanceModel.test.mjs` |

| A removal says what happened to the folder in the node's own word: one asked to go that could not is *still on disk*, with where and why, never *in the Trash* (`removeProjectModel.removedSaid`); only a folder the workspace made is ever offered for deletion — a record nobody can read counts as adopted (`isAdopted`) | `views/_work/removeProjectModel.test.mjs` |
| The Board's day is a calendar day — across a month, a leap day, a year and a clock change, at both ends of midnight; a due date nobody can read is no date; a thousand cards land in rank order inside a second, and a card set down in its own place is the same board | `views/_board/boardModel.test.mjs` |

| The rail's time is the total while live and the run's length once ended: a session's `started` (presence, never reset), a sub-agent's own `started` (its spawn instant, the same rule) and a terminal's `openedAt`/`exitedAt` anchor it, `durationPrecise` reads it, and the state timer moves to the tooltip; while live the counter counts from `pulse.liveStart` in a `<LiveDuration>` leaf, so it advances without rebuilding the rail model; a session whose claiming tab exited is ended as of the exit (`settledByTab`) and nothing of it counts up | `views/_workbench/workstreamPulseModel.test.mjs`, `i18n/format.test.mjs`, `shell/terminalsModel.test.mjs` |
